## ⚠ READ THIS BEFORE BUILDING ANY OF THEM

**Not one of the ten came back sound.** 4 CONTRADICT the code, 6 NEED WORK. Treat every spec
below as a DRAFT whose defect list is the more valuable half.

**The most important finding: TWO agents (qbrt, sixstrum) independently invented the SAME false
blocker** — that PF-6f cannot ship because `PatchEngine` is reachable only through Svelte
context, therefore a shell-cells `action` needs a ~14 LOC platform change first. That is wrong.
`packages/web/src/lib/audio/engine-ref.ts` already exports `getActiveEngine()` for exactly this,
and it is ALREADY consumed from plain `.ts` modules (`clipplayer.ts:28`,
`push2-control.svelte.ts:35`). Both specs would have shipped an unnecessary prerequisite PR.
Because two independent agents made the identical error, assume a THIRD would too — this is a
systematic misreading of the engine seam, not a one-off.

## qbrt

**Verdict: CONTRADICTS_CODE** · archetype: DUAL-IDENTITY PROCESSOR — a stereo resonant filter you insert (always-on, dual-mono, no cross-feed) that a rising edge on PING turns into a struck percussion VOICE. Four params, one audition action, one glyph. It is the only module in Batch B whose two identities share the same four knobs: CUTOFF is simultaneously the corner frequency and the pitch the resonator rings at (qbrt.ts:47-48, qbrt.dsp:66-72).

**Rationale (as authored):** WHAT IT IS FOR, in one paragraph: qbrt is TWO INSTRUMENTS SHARING FOUR KNOBS, and the face has never said so. Insert it and it is the project's big-knob stereo VCF — strictly dual-mono, four two-pole taps computed in parallel per channel and crossfaded by one continuous MODE knob (qbrt.dsp:39-53), with a Q of 0.7–20.5 that is NOT gain-compensated and, being a plain two-pole section, NEVER self-oscillates. Send it a rising edge on PING and it becomes a struck percussion voice with no oscillator in the patch: a ~1 ms broadband click into both filter inputs plus a +30 Q slam that decays with PING DEC (qbrt.dsp:59-73). The verb a player performs is different for each identity — you RIDE cutoff on the filter, you STRIKE the resonator — and the module has never carried a strike affordance on ANY surface.

WHY THIS `order`. Ranks 1-3 are unchanged and are right, and the plan-note is correct that they are right BY ACCIDENT — the existing array is declaration order. They now have arguments, and each argument would be WRONG for a different module (the Step-3 bar):
 • CUTOFF at 1 because it is the ONLY control that is two things at once: the corner frequency of the insert AND the pitch the resonator rings at (qbrt.ts:47-48). On the mini tile that single knob is the whole module in both identities. On a filter that could self-oscillate, RESONANCE would compete for this slot; this one cannot, so it does not.
 • RESONANCE at 2 because it is not gain-compensated (qbrt.dsp:69, `qBase = res*20 + 0.7`, no output scaling): it is simultaneously the sharpness, the ring length AND the loudest thing on the module (≈ +26 dB at the top, qbrt.ts:182). On a Q-compensated filter this would be a timbre knob and would rank below MODE.
 • MODE at 3 because it decides WHICH of four responses you are hearing — but only one at a time, and only after CUTOFF has decided where. It is a character switch, not something you ride.
 • PING DEC at 4 because it is INERT AT SPAWN in the shipped module (nothing pings) and it is not even the whole tail — the ring's own decay is set by RESONANCE and CUTOFF (qbrt.ts:186).
 • `qbrt-ping-{n}` at 5, appended: the second instrument's ON-RAMP, and the reason PING DEC stops being inert. It is inside the 6-cell lane budget on purpose — an unpatched qbrt in the lane should not be a mute rectangle on the one module whose headline capability needs no oscillator.

WHY THESE `pages`. Two bands, unchanged in count, both headers rewritten to TEACH the fact a patcher most needs and cannot infer from the jacks. `filter · dual mono` states the thing that is invisible on the rear: L and R are identical banks off ONE knob set, with no cross-feed and no M/S, so the stereo image you patch in survives untouched (qbrt.ts:159). `ping · plays the filter` states the thing that is invisible in the DSP: the filter never self-oscillates, so PING is the ONLY way to make it sing (qbrt.ts:182). Neither band is merged and neither is split — the merge/split question does not arise, because these are two genuinely different ideas and the 720p fold is not binding here (the committed dock overflow for qbrt is 18 px past the 425 px cap, vrt-exemptions.ts:1031, so the band budget was never the constraint the batch specs assumed).

WHY `order` AND `pages` DISAGREE, deliberately: `order` answers "if this tier shows only N, which N?"; `pages` answers "in what order does signal flow?". The action ranks LAST in priority (you press it once; you ride the knobs) and FIRST in function (the trigger causes, the decay shapes). Same platform rule that resolved snaredrum and sixstrum. Do not reconcile them.

WHY THE REAR CHANGES ONLY ITS LABELS. `signal` → `stereo in · no cross-feed` puts the dual-mono fact on the jack field where the patching decision is made, in the house `·` style already precedented at 34 characters (mixer's `channel inputs · in1→ch1 … in4→ch4`). The plan's proposed cluster is REJECTED for the reason the plan itself gives: a cluster over a two-hole band covers 100 % of it, producing an empty band header immediately followed by a cluster header — the rear analogue of the single-control page. `audioRate` stays EMPTY, and here is the Step-6 citation the recipe demands: every knob is `si.smoo`-ed inside the DSP at a ≈7 Hz corner (qbrt.dsp:6-11) and the Faust wrapper block-samples `parameters[path][0]` once per 128-frame quantum (≈375 Hz at 48 kHz, qbrt.ts:21-25). Not one of the four CV jacks is audio-rate. qbrt correctly ticks nothing.

WHAT I AM NOT DOING, and why. No new ParamDef (the def is already 1:1 with the DSP's four hsliders — a finding, not a shortfall). No `response` glyph (demoted, with qbrt named as the reason). No PF-1 Segmented on `mode` (it would hide the blends AND fail the vocabulary gate). No `strike` press-param (it would cost a contract line and persist a one-shot into the Y.Doc). No CV-scale change on the `mode` input, even though its `discrete` bucketing to the two ends of a four-tap morph (qbrt.ts:170) is arguably the strangest thing in the module — that is a contract change and belongs in its own PR, never folded into a face wave.

VERIFICATION GATE for this PR, in order: `task test:one -- module-face-lint` · `-- shell-cells` · `-- param-vocabulary` · `-- module-docs-lint` · `-- rear-card-model` · `-- vrt-meta` · `task docs:check` · `REPEAT=3 npx --workspace e2e playwright test faces-parity --grep qbrt` · `REPEAT=3 task e2e:one -- qbrt-ping-audition` · `REPEAT=3 task e2e:one -- workflow-shell-faces` · `REPEAT=3 task vrt:one -- qbrt` (inspect BOTH PNG diffs; face-qbrt-compact must be clean) · `task typecheck`. The new audition spec must carry a NEGATIVE CONTROL — assert the scope peak is ~0 BEFORE the click and > 0.005 after (the threshold is a measured precedent, not a guess: coverage-groups-6-7-8-9.spec.ts:122 already uses exactly 0.005 for qbrt's L output at cutoff 400 / resonance 0.8 / pingDecay 0.1). Without the before-assert, a scope tap reading some other node's audio would pass a dead button — the instrument would be blind to the very dimension under test.

```ts
face: {
  order: ["cutoff", "resonance", "mode", "pingDecay", "qbrt-ping-{n}"],
  pages: [
    { id: "filter", label: "filter \u00b7 dual mono", controls: ["cutoff", "resonance", "mode"] },
    { id: "ping", label: "ping \u00b7 plays the filter", controls: ["qbrt-ping-{n}", "pingDecay"] },
  ],
  glyph: "scope \u2014 UNCHANGED (qbrt.ts:137). Keep it, and keep the def's existing 8-line justification comment (qbrt.ts:124-130) verbatim: the module's identity is the RING (a decaying sinusoid whose pitch is CUTOFF and whose length is RESONANCE \u00d7 PING DEC), which a trace shows and an RMS meter does not. The def's own wish for a param-derived FREQUENCY-RESPONSE glyph stays unfulfilled: the plan DEMOTES the `response` glyph kind and names qbrt as the reason (plan:118 \u2014 \"qbrt's `mode` is a continuous 0..1 morph\", so it needs its own law, which is the special-casing the new-kind argument existed to avoid). Do not revive it here. \u26a0 MEASURED FACT the plan's global glyph-cliff warning gets wrong for this module: qbrt ALREADY renders NO in-lane glyph at the `full` tier. laneBodyPlan(4, true, 'full') (module-shell-model.ts:328-343) \u2192 4 > LANE_ROW_MAX_CELLS_WITH_GLYPH(2) \u2192 plate; cellCount = min(4,6) = 4; rows = ceil(4/3) = 2; `glyph = hasGlyph && rows <= 1` = FALSE. Going 4 \u2192 5 cells keeps rows at 2, so this face pays NO glyph price for the new rank. The glyph still renders at mini, at compact (2 cells + fluid trace) and as the dock hero (DOCK_HERO_GLYPH_W = 214 px).",
  paramCells: none — qbrt declares no `face.paramCells`. `'grid'` is the only legal value (graph/types.ts:566-582) and it is dx7-scoped (a chart of 32 pictures). `mode` is a CONTINUOUS morph, so its primitive stays a KnobConic at every tier — param-vocabulary.test.ts:194-201 asserts exactly that ("landmarks do NOT change the primitive — they only decorate the dial"). Adding a paramCells entry would also fail module-face-lint.test.ts:546 ("every face.paramCells key is a ranked, non-momentary, DISCRETE param"). `face.momentary` is likewise absent — the PING audition is a FAMILY/action cell, not a press-param, deliberately: a `strike` ParamDef would be a contract line AND would put a persisted 0/1 in the Y.Doc for a one-shot.,
}
```

**New params:**
- ZERO new ParamDefs. Step-8 finding, stated as a finding rather than a shortfall: qbrt's four params are 1:1 with the DSP's four `hslider`s (qbrt.dsp:6-11 cutoff/resonance/mode/pingDecay ↔ qbrt.ts:100-103). There is NO un-exposed DSP capability. Every remaining constant in the .dsp is genuinely fixed by design — the click's 0.98 per-sample coefficient (qbrt.dsp:33, ~1 ms), its 1.5 peak amplitude (:72), and the +30 Q boost (:70). Exposing any of them would be a Faust recompile + an ART re-pin and must NEVER be folded into a face wave (plan Step 8).
- ADD `landmarks` to the EXISTING `mode` param (PF-10, already shipped — see risks). Exact declaration, values read off qbrt.dsp:44-52: landmarks: [{value: 0, label: 'LP'}, {value: 1/3, label: 'BP'}, {value: 2/3, label: 'HP'}, {value: 1, label: 'X-BP'}]. WHY: `m3 = mSm*3`, `seg = int(min(2,m3))`, `t = m3-seg`, and `ba.selectn` crossfades lp→bp, bp→hp, hp→notch where `notch = x - bp`. So the four taps sit exactly at 0, ⅓, ⅔, 1 and everything between is a real linear blend. Range/default untouched (0..1 linear, default 0 → the dial boots reading 'LP').
- WHY LANDMARKS AND NOT PF-1 `options`: two independent reasons. (i) Semantics — the blends ARE the feature; the def's own docs sell them ('0.5 sits half band-pass, half high-pass', qbrt.ts:184), and a Segmented would hide two thirds of the control. (ii) The gate forbids it — param-vocabulary.test.ts:71-81 requires `curve: 'discrete'` for any param carrying `options`, and `mode` is `curve: 'linear'` (qbrt.ts:102). Declaring `options` fails the unit lane immediately.
- WHY THE 4th LABEL IS `X-BP` AND NOT `NOTCH`: `NOTCH` would be a LIE at the shipped default `resonance` 0.7. The def's own docs (qbrt.ts:184) state the tap only truly nulls near resonance ≈ 0.015, is flat by ≈ 0.065, and above that INVERTS into a ≈ +23 dB resonant PEAK. `X-BP` mirrors the DSP's literal `notch = x - bp` (qbrt.dsp:47) and the docs' phrase 'the input-minus-band-pass tap'. ASCII hyphen deliberately, not U+2212: VRT pins a webfont (e2e/vrt/_fonts.ts) and a minus-sign glyph risks tofu in the 9 px mono readout (KnobConic.svelte:411-424).
- ADD one ControlFamily (PF-6f): { id: 'qbrt-ping', label: 'Ping (audition)', kind: 'other', testidPrefix: 'qbrt-ping' }. This is the ONLY added contract line. Modelled on dx7's one-member families (dx7.ts:317-318).

**Control families:**
- qbrt-ping

**Control-loss ledger:**
- ZERO control loss from the legacy card, verified line by line. QbrtCard.svelte is 41 lines total: one `.stripe`, one `<ModuleTitle>`, one `<PatchPanel>` wrapping four `<Fader>`s (QbrtCard.svelte:30-33). No `<button>`, no `<select>`, no `<input>`, no `oncontextmenu`, no `format`, no name table, no preset roster. All four params are ranked and all four render in the dock. The Step-7 adversarial grep returns nothing.
- NOT A LOSS — A NAMING DIVERGENCE, state it in the PR body: the shell paints the DEF's labels, so the dock/lane read `Res` and `Ping Dec` (qbrt.ts:101,103) where the legacy card reads `Resonance` and `Ping Decay` (QbrtCard.svelte:31,33). This is deliberate and already shipped — the def comment at qbrt.ts:92-98 says the def labels were authored FOR the face and the rear holes. Do NOT 'fix' the card to import them; that would move the STRICT-lane card baseline for a cosmetic.
- PING AUDITION — a NEW capability on BOTH surfaces, not a recovered loss. Correcting the plan's §5 ledger row (plan:1191, 'MODERATE — the headline capability is untriggerable from the dock'): it is untriggerable from the CARD too. QbrtCard.svelte has no strike button and qbrt.ts's handle (qbrt.ts:214-242) declares no `read()`. Lands in this PR on BOTH surfaces: shell → `qbrt-ping-{n}` action cell; card → a `data-testid="qbrt-ping"` button. The card half is NOT optional (see contractDelta risk 1).
- `mode` LP/BP/HP/X-BP names — never existed on either surface. New, via PF-10 landmarks. Not a loss.
- NOTHING is deliberately dropped, and NOTHING moves to a dock-only rank. All five ranks sit inside the 6-cell lane budget (PF-7), so every control qbrt has reaches the `full` in-lane plate.

**CI delta (claimed):** ≈ +13 s total, itemized. (a) faces-parity `qbrt` row: 4 → 5 cells × the plan's MEASURED ~0.8 s/cell on CI = +0.8 s. (b) The new `e2e/tests/qbrt-ping-audition.spec.ts`: one `?shell=1` boot (the spec-fixed cost faces-parity already pays, ~6-9 s cold on a SwiftShader runner) + spawn + dock open + a 400 ms capture ≈ +10-14 s. (c) The landmark ticks, the rear/page label edits and the def edits are unit-lane only: ~0. Well under the 2-minute owner sign-off bar (`ci-walltime-2min-approval`) — STATE IT IN THE PR BODY ANYWAY and CONFIRM ON CI, not locally: CI runs SwiftShader at roughly 4× the local per-cell cost, and this spec's cost is dominated by the shell boot, not by the audio capture. If (b) measures over ~20 s on CI, fold the assertion into the existing rack-mode harness instead (drive the CARD button, reusing the `rack` fixture that `coverage-groups-6-7-8-9.spec.ts:89` already uses) and accept that the shell action path stays click-only.

**Risks (as authored):**
- ⚠⚠ BLOCKER — PF-6f AS THE PLAN DESCRIBES IT CANNOT WORK. The plan says the audition is '+1 family + a shell-cells `action` spec' (plan:105,670). It is not. `ShellActionCell.onFire` is typed `(nodeId: string) => void` (shell-cells.ts:62-67) and ModuleShell calls it as `cell.onFire(id)` (ModuleShell.svelte:584). A ping audition is an ENGINE READ — `engine.read(node,'manualTrigger')` (engine.ts:2147, the karplus.ts:288-295 / sixstrum.ts:379 / samsloop.ts:1135 seam) — and the PatchEngine is reachable ONLY through Svelte context (`provideEngineContext` at Canvas.svelte:621, `useEngine()` at card-kit.ts:96). A plain function in shell-cells.ts has no context. An action cell as shipped can only mutate the graph store. REQUIRED PLATFORM DELTA (~14 LOC, 2 files): add `readKey?: string` to ShellActionCell, make `onFire` optional, and in ModuleShell's action branch resolve `params.engineCtx.get()` + `patch.nodes[id]` → `e.read(live, cell.readKey)` and invoke the returned function (the shell already does exactly this pair of lookups in `firePressParam`, ModuleShell.svelte:347-353). Add a shell-cells.test clause: an `action` cell declares EXACTLY ONE of `onFire` / `readKey`. This is not qbrt scope creep — it pre-implements PF-6's own `readKey` field and unblocks karplus, sixstrum, kickdrum and snaredrum identically. Ship it in the qbrt PR or in a 1-file platform PR immediately before it.
- ⚠⚠ faces-parity's `action` branch (faces-parity.spec.ts:506-511) asserts ONLY `toBeEnabled()` and then clicks. IT ASSERTS NO EFFECT. A dead PING button — wrong readKey, missing `read()` on the handle, a ConstantSource that was never `.start()`-ed — passes the whole face green. This is precisely the class the PANEL probe rule was written to kill (shell-cells.ts:113-131: 'a revision-only probe passes on a DEAD button'), and the `action` kind has NO probe at all. CONSEQUENCE: the bespoke audition spec below is MANDATORY, not a nicety. FOLLOW-UP (own PR, not this one): give `ShellActionCell` an optional `probe` mirroring `ShellPanelProbe` so every future audition cell auto-enrols.
- ⚠ THE LEGACY CARD MUST GAIN THE BUTTON — it is a gate requirement, not a nicety, and it is what makes this PR touch the REQUIRED vrt-strict lane. module-docs-lint.test.ts:232-247 greps every declared `testidPrefix` against `allCardSource()`, which walks `packages/web/src/lib/ui/**/*.svelte` (:76-88). ModuleShell DELIBERATELY does not satisfy it: `cellTestId` returns `shell-cell-<familyId>` and its comment (ModuleShell.svelte:373-378) says 'Deliberately NOT the family's own testidPrefix (that id belongs to the legacy card and is grep-pinned to it by the docs gate)'. So `qbrt-ping` must appear as a real card testid — the KarplusCard.svelte:120-129 / SixstrumCard.svelte:188-194 precedent. That is what moves both `vrt.spec.ts` qbrt baselines.
- ⚠ qbrt is on INTERACTIVE_DOC_MODULES (strict-docs.ts:222-226; probed by docs-virtual-module.spec.ts:466-472, 'qbrt cutoff→cutoff'). The doc-page sandbox mounts the REAL QbrtCard with `provideEngineContext(() => null)` (VirtualModule.svelte:74). The new handler MUST guard `const e = engineCtx.get(); if (!e || !node) return;` — copy KarplusCard.svelte:39-48 verbatim. Without the guard the docs spec throws on mount. (Buttons are fine for interactive eligibility — writeseq's card buttons are the precedent, docs-virtual-module.spec.ts:441-450.)
- THE PLAN CONTRADICTS ITSELF ON `order`. plan:657 says '`order` **unchanged** (`cutoff, resonance, mode, pingDecay`)' while plan:670 in the same bullet list adds a `qbrt-ping` controlFamily. module-face-lint.test.ts:226-230 fails any STRICT_FACES module with a declared family absent from `face.order` ('control family X not in face.order (need X-{n})'). `order` MUST gain a 5th key. What IS unchanged — and what the plan-note's 'right by accident' point actually protects — is the RELATIVE order of the four knobs, which this spec leaves byte-identical and APPENDS to.
- THE BRIEF'S PAGE-ID/REAR-GROUP-ID RULE IS WRONG AS STATED, and qbrt is the counter-example that proves it. rear-card-model.ts:287-291: for each page, a curated group whose id EQUALS the page id CLAIMS that page's slot (its label wins) and `usedGroupIds.add(g.id)` stops the extra-curated-groups loop at :303-304 from emitting it again. That is the DESIGNED merge — qbrt SHIPS this collision today (page `ping` ↔ rear group `ping`, qbrt.ts:135,152) and the band renders ONCE. The double-render happens only when the curated group id is `'voice'` or `'signal'`, because the voice-slot claim at :262-269 fires UNCONDITIONALLY, before and independently of the page loop — which is exactly dx7's case and exactly why dx7 had to use `id:'patch'` with `label:'voice'`. CONCRETE RULE FOR QBRT: keep rear group ids `signal` and `ping`; keep page ids `filter` and `ping`; NEVER rename the `filter` page id to `signal` (that would double-render the L/R band and the totality gate would NOT catch it — every port still renders once).
- PAGE LABEL ↔ REAR BAND LABEL COUPLING. The rear band for the `filter` page derives its header from `page.label` (rear-card-model.ts:291), while the rear band for the `ping` page takes the CURATED group's label (:288-290). So renaming the `ping` PAGE label without renaming the `ping` REAR GROUP label desynchronises the two faces of the same band. Both are specified above as the same string; change them together or not at all. This is the class rear-card-model.test.ts pins for six other modules — qbrt has NO pin there (verified: zero `qbrt` hits in that file), so nothing will catch a desync.
- LATENT SINGLE-SOURCE-OF-TRUTH VIOLATION IN THE CARD — report it, and fix it while we are in the file. QbrtCard.svelte:30-33 re-types all TWELVE range numbers (`min={20} max={20000} defaultValue={1000}`, etc.) instead of reading `qbrtDef.params`, even though it already imports `qbrtDef` (:5). Worse, :14-17 index the param array POSITIONALLY (`qbrtDef.params[0]!.defaultValue`), so reordering the def's params silently rebinds every default. I VERIFIED all twelve numbers currently AGREE with qbrt.ts:100-103 — so this is a HAZARD, not a live bug, and I am NOT reporting it as a defect. But it is exactly the class CLAUDE.md's 'A CARD can silently disagree with its DEF' section names, and the card is the one qbrt surface no def-reading gate can see. Boy-scout fix in this PR: source min/max/defaultValue from `qbrtDef.params.find(p => p.id === …)`. SCOPE IT TO THE NUMBERS ONLY — do NOT source `label` from the def, or the card's 'Resonance'/'Ping Decay' become 'Res'/'Ping Dec' and the strict-lane baseline moves for a cosmetic.
- THE ORDER-4-vs-5 TASTE CALL, with its revert. I rank `qbrt-ping-{n}` FIFTH (append) and leave `pingDecay` fourth. The counter-argument is real and is the repo's own bar: Step 3 says check every hero candidate for INERTNESS AT SPAWN, and `pingDecay` is inert at spawn (nothing pings) while the audition button is fully live — which argues the button should precede the knob it makes meaningful. I append anyway, for three reasons: (i) the plan-note's whole instruction is that the four-knob order is right and must not be re-presented as a redesign, and appending leaves it byte-identical; (ii) NO TIER RENDERS A 4-CELL PREFIX (mini=1, compact=2, full=min(5,6)=5, dock=all), so the choice changes nothing that renders — only the plate's reading order; (iii) in the 3×2 plate, row 2 reads [PING DEC][▸] rather than [▸][PING DEC], and the labelled knob before the unlabelled action chip is the better read (the shell renders an action as a bare '▸' at lane tiers, ModuleShell.svelte:581). ONE-LINE REVERT: swap ranks 4 and 5 in `face.order`. Nothing else moves — `pages` already puts the action FIRST in the ping band.
- `order` AND `pages` DISAGREE HERE ON PURPOSE — put this in the face comment or the next author will 'fix' it. `order` ends `… pingDecay, qbrt-ping-{n}` (PRIORITY: you ride knobs, you press an audition once). `pages[1]` reads `qbrt-ping-{n}, pingDecay` (FUNCTION: the trigger causes, the decay shapes what it caused). Verified harmless to the rear: the page-band derivation only looks up per-param CVs keyed by param id (rear-card-model.ts:292-298), so a family key contributes nothing and the rear band stays [PING jack, PING DEC cv] either way.
- TRIGGER-PULSE / EDGE-DETECTOR COMPATIBILITY — verified, not assumed. `fireTrigger` defaults to a 5 ms triangle (TRIGGER_PULSE_S = 0.005, gate-trigger.ts:35,53-69): 0 → 1 over 2.5 ms, back to 0 over 2.5 ms. qbrt.dsp:14's `edge(x) = (x >= 0.5) & (x' < 0.5)` is per-sample inside the Faust worklet, so it sees roughly 2.4 ms above GATE_HI and fires EXACTLY ONCE, then re-arms. No `createEdgeCounter` is involved (that rule is for MAIN-THREAD AnalyserNode rescans; a per-sample worklet detector is correct by construction, CLAUDE.md). The new ConstantSource sums into `merger` input 2 ALONGSIDE the existing `silence` keep-alive (qbrt.ts:194-199), which sits at offset 0 — additive, and the keep-alive is preserved (unlike sixstrum, whose normalling detection forbids keep-alives; qbrt has no such invariant, it is a plain audio merger).
- ART: NO re-pin, and no `.sha` exists to move. Confirmed against plan:1287 and the repo layout — qbrt has no `art/scenarios/qbrt/` pin, and it is NOT one of the five defs whose ART profile hashes the def SOURCE (`docsStrippedRepoSourceSha`: delay/scaler/polarizer/illogic/depolarizer, plan:98). So PF-11 is irrelevant here and a face edit is free. ZERO DSP change: no `.dsp` edit, no Faust recompile, no `.f32` movement — the audition is entirely host-side, the kickdrum/snaredrum rule (plan:1286).
- ATTEST: NIL. qbrt is an AUDIO def (no `docs-hash-ignore` markers needed), `shell-cells.ts` / `ModuleShell.svelte` / `graph/types.ts` are in neither the collab nor the grand basis. THREE WAYS TO BREAK THAT ACCIDENTALLY, all avoidable and all in play here: (i) do NOT append anything to `e2e/tests/_drivers.ts` (every append flips the collab hash); (ii) do NOT edit `e2e/tests/_helpers.ts` — IMPORT `spawnPatch` from it, never modify it; (iii) do NOT write the literal `@collab` anywhere in the new spec — the collab basis tag-grep matches the @-form and would pull the file in (`collab-basis-tag-grep-footgun`). A brand-new `e2e/tests/*.spec.ts` is otherwise attest-neutral (it is not in `e2e/webgl-heavy-globs.ts`'s list, and e2e/tests/** is out of the WebGL hash).

**Counts independently re-derived by the fact-checker:**

> Independently derived from packages/web/src/lib/audio/modules/qbrt.ts: 4 params (cutoff, resonance, mode, pingDecay — qbrt.ts:100-103); 7 inputs (L, R, ping, cutoff, resonance, mode, pingDecay — qbrt.ts:73-86); 2 outputs (L, R — qbrt.ts:89-90) = 9 ports. 2 face.pages (filter, ping — qbrt.ts:134-135), 2 face.rear.groups (signal, ping — qbrt.ts:151-152), face.order length 4 (qbrt.ts:132), glyph 'scope' (qbrt.ts:137), no paramCells, no momentary, no controlFamilies. contract-lock.txt qbrt block = 14 lines (2560-2573: 1 meta + 7 in + 2 out + 4 param). ALL of these AGREE with the spec's numbers — its param/port/cell/contract arithmetic (14 -> 15 lines, ranks 1-5 inside the 6-cell PF-7 budget, no invented param ids, no silently dropped param, card's 12 hardcoded range numbers matching the def, page id 'ping' colliding with rear group id 'ping' yet merging once per rear-card-model.ts:288-290, and the full-tier glyph already being FALSE at 4 cells via laneBodyPlan) all check out. The defects below are elsewhere.

**DEFECTS — fix these before building:**

- **[BLOCKER]** risks[0] declares a BLOCKER — 'PF-6f AS THE PLAN DESCRIBES IT CANNOT WORK ... the PatchEngine is reachable ONLY through Svelte context ... An action cell as shipped can only mutate the graph store. REQUIRED PLATFORM DELTA (~14 LOC, 2 files): add readKey?: string to ShellActionCell ... Ship it in the qbrt PR or in a 1-file platform PR immediately before it.' This is false. A non-context, process-wide engine accessor already exists and is already used from plain .ts modules.
  - *evidence:* packages/web/src/lib/audio/engine-ref.ts:3-5 — 'A tiny process-wide accessor for the live PatchEngine, for code that runs OUTSIDE the Svelte context tree where provideEngineContext / useEngine apply'; :23-25 `export function getActiveEngine(): PatchEngine | null`. It is live in the shell: Canvas.svelte:6991 `setActiveEngine(e)` (cleared at :7556). It is ALREADY consumed from non-component .ts modules exactly as a shell-cells `onFire(nodeId)` would: packages/web/src/lib/audio/modules/clipplayer.ts:28 + :794/:810/:2097/:2239, and packages/web/src/lib/control/push2/push2-control.svelte.ts:35 + :279 (`const e = getActiveEngine();`). Combined with engine.ts:2147 `read(node, key)` and the karplus.ts:288-295 `manualTrigger` seam, a shell-cells `action` spec can fire the audition today with zero platform change — which is precisely what the plan asserts at plan:105 ('The no-platform-change FALLBACK, which ships TODAY'). The spec's own cited evidence (shell-cells.ts:62-67 onFire signature, ModuleShell.svelte:584 `cell.onFire(id)`) is accurate but does not support the conclusion: onFire receives the nodeId, and the nodeId is all getActiveEngine()+patch.nodes need. The prescribed ShellActionCell.readKey field, the optional-onFire change, the ModuleShell action-branch rewrite and the new shell-cells.test 'exactly one of onFire/readKey' clause are all unnecessary scope, and the spec instructs shipping them as a prerequisite PR.

- **[MAJOR]** The spec ships the exact page-label <-> rear-band-label desync its own risks[6] warns about. It renames the `ping` PAGE label to 'ping · plays the filter' but never respecifies the `ping` REAR GROUP label, then asserts they are the same string.

## delay

**Verdict: NEEDS_WORK** · archetype: mono insert FX — one audio in, one audio out, one param-targeted CV jack. The "processor" face archetype shared with reverb / shimmershine / cloudseed / filter (glyph `meter`, curated rear `signal` band labelled `mono in`, house `output blend` tail page). delay is the primitive of that family: no sync, no tone, no stereo, no feedback CV. What separates it from every sibling is that its hero knob is the only one that makes a SOUND OF ITS OWN — the DelayNode is a fractional-read line, so moving TIME varispeeds the buffer and Dopplers the tail (measured, delay.ts:10-17: +0.5 s ramp over 1 s drops a 1 kHz sine to ~498 Hz = the exact varispeed prediction, nowhere near the 1000 Hz a crossfading line would hold).

**Rationale (as authored):** WHAT IT IS FOR (recipe Step 2). Delay is the rack's plainest echo — the one you patch when you want repeats and nothing else. Every sibling is a decoration of it: COFEFVE adds the tape machine, CHARLOTTE'S ECHOS adds destruction, reverb/cloudseed diffuse instead of repeat. So its face cannot be sold on features; it has to be sold on the ONE thing it does that none of them do, which is that TIME IS A PERFORMANCE CONTROL. The DelayNode is a fractional-read line, so moving TIME varispeeds the buffer and Dopplers the tail (delay.ts:10-17, measured in headless Chromium: a +0.5 s ramp over 1 s drops a 1 kHz sine to ~498 Hz — the exact varispeed prediction). The verb a player performs on this module is RIDE TIME; feedback and mix are set.

WHY THIS `order` (Step 3 — every rank defended by an argument that would be WRONG for a different module).

1 `time`. On reverb, cloudseed, shimmershine, COFEFVE, the time-ish control is a SETTING. Here it is an audio event: turning it bends the pitch of everything already in the buffer, and under CV it is unsmoothed and audio-rate. It is also the only knob that sets the RHYTHM — what the echo plays against. Both halves of that argument are delay-specific.

2 `feedback`. It decides WHAT KIND of echo this is — one slap, eight repeats, or a 135-repeat wash (delay.ts:63-70, :174). It is also the only control on the module that cannot be done anywhere else: this is a rack, and every module already has a fader in MIXMSTRS, so 'how loud is the wet' is available downstream. 'How many repeats' is not. So feedback beats mix.

3 `mix`. The OUTPUT STAGE, and the least structural of the three — it changes how much you hear, not what you hear. Equal-power (delay.ts:185-193, :253-257) so it is not even level-critical the way reverb's linear crossfade is (reverb.ts:99 leads with `mix` for exactly that reason — an argument delay does NOT get to borrow).

4 `time_cv_amt`. It is INERT AT SPAWN — with no cable in the TIME jack, the attenuator multiplies nothing. Recipe Step 3 makes inertness disqualifying for a hero slot (the tidyVco `pw` / shimmershine `decay` / karplus `position` class; qbrt's `pingDecay` is ranked 4 on the same ground), so it cannot outrank a control that always does something. Rank 4 is not a compromise, it is the rule.

WHY `pages` IS ONE BAND, AND WHY IT MAY DISAGREE WITH `order` (Step 4). `order` is a PRIORITY ranking for tiers that show a subset; `pages` is FUNCTION order for the tier that shows everything. Here they happen to agree, and the face comment must SAY they are allowed not to, or the next author will 'fix' one of them.

The old two-band split does not survive being questioned. `output blend` held ONE knob and is a house template copy-pasted across four modules — cloudseed.ts:626 (4 controls), reverb.ts:99 (1), shimmershine.ts:271 (1), delay.ts:134 (1). Three of the four are single-knob bands, which is the tell that it was inherited rather than decided. Per the plan's own rule, a page earns a header only if it groups ≥2 controls or the lone control is the module's identity; `mix` is neither. And per §1 the merge must be justified by GROUPING SEMANTICS, not budget: time, feedback and mix are three knobs of ONE idea — the recirculating line and how much of it you hear. Every delay pedal ever made puts them in one row. Band label `echo · time · feedback · mix` follows the house style this program already set for a collapsed band (the plan's lfo entry: `rate · depth · shape`).

WHY A CLUSTER, NOT A SECOND PAGE, FOR `time_cv_amt`. It IS a different idea — it is a trim on a JACK, the only front control whose meaning is 'how hard does the cable on the back push' — so it wants a name. But a one-control PAGE is exactly what the `output blend` collapse just convicted, and it costs an ~81 px band for one knob. `ModuleFacePage.clusters` (PF-9) exists for precisely this trade: ~14 px of sub-header instead of ~81 px of band (graph/types.ts:495-503). So: one page, one cluster labelled `time cv depth`, membership staying in `page.controls` as the type requires. TASTE CALL, one-line revert: delete `clusters` and it renders four flat knobs.

WHY THE REAR MUST BE PINNED (Step 5 — re-derived on paper against rear-card-model.ts, not assumed). Today `time` derives into the page band `line`/'delay line' (rear-card-model.ts:285-299), which reads fine. Collapse the pages and it would derive into a band named after the `echo` page — worse than today, and the plan is right that the collapse FORCES the pin. Add `{ id: 'mod', label: 'time cv · varispeeds the line', ports: ['time'] }`. Because 'mod' matches no page id it appends after the (now empty, therefore dropped) page band, giving exactly two bands: `signal`/'mono in' [AUDIO] and `mod` [TIME ~], plus the AUDIO output rail. 3 declared ports = 3 holes; the totality gate (module-face-lint.test.ts:749-777) balances. Keep `audioRate: ['time']` — the claim is still TRUE under PF-12 and I re-checked the whole chain per Step 6: WaveShaper (a-rate) → attenuate GainNode (a-rate) → `DelayNode.delayTime` (a-rate AudioParam), per-sample end to end. Update the face comment's citation to name the added gain, or the next reader will think the tick was never re-verified.

NO NEW SHELL CELL IS NEEDED. Every ranked key is a param, so `shellCellFor` returns null for all four (shell-cells.ts:240-243) and the shell paints them generically. `SHELL_CELLS` (:161-232) registers only dx7 and sixstrum and needs no delay entry — nothing here can render INERT.

THE BUG I FOUND (report it even though it is not live). DelayCard.svelte:33-35 RE-TYPES every range and default as literals — `min={0.001} max={2} defaultValue={0.25}`, `min={0} max={0.95} defaultValue={0.4}`, `min={0} max={1} defaultValue={0.35}` — while reading the def for the initial VALUE on the very next lines up (:18-20 `?? delayDef.params[0]!.defaultValue`). So the card reads the def for one half of each param and hardcodes the other half. I checked all nine numbers against delay.ts:92-94 (and `MAX_DELAY_S = 2` at :61, `MAX_FEEDBACK = 0.95` at :70): they AGREE today. This is NOT the backdraft ±0.2-vs-±1 bug — it is the same CLASS, one edit away, and it is invisible to every gate we have (contract-lock, module-docs-lint and all the face lints read the DEF; the card is never compared to it). CLAUDE.md's rule is that a control's range must come from ONE place, and today it cannot: `MAX_DELAY_S`/`MAX_FEEDBACK` are module-private `const`s, not exported. Three-line boy-scout fix belongs in this PR: `export` both constants and drive the Faders off `delayDef.params[i].{min,max,defaultValue}`.

CORRECTIONS TO THE PLAN'S PROSE, with file:line.
(1) §4.B opens '**PF-11 FIRST** … `delay.ts` wraps `docs:` but not `face:` — so even a pure face edit moves the `.sha` today.' **ALREADY DONE.** delay.ts:97 opens a `docs-hash-ignore:start` that closes at :154 and wraps the ENTIRE `face:` block (:130-153), with the PF-11 rationale authored in the comment at :98-108. A pure face edit is already free. What still moves the `.sha` in THIS PR is the new ParamDef (:85-95) and the factory (:181-275), both outside the markers — a different reason, and PF-11 does not help with it.
(2) '**Needs PF-12 (with the tap move)**' is correct that PF-12 is required, but the plan never mentions that `filter` ALREADY SHIPPED two attenuverters of exactly this kind with no platform change (filter.ts:118-119, :229-236, :253-255). That omission is dangerous, because the obvious move — 'copy filter' — silently changes delay's CV behaviour (see risks). The mechanisms differ because filter's CV inputs are plain node inputs and delay's is an AudioParam target.
(3) §6's VRT table lists only DRAINS for Batch B and omits delay's linux RE-CAPTURE entirely (see vrtImpact). This is the item most likely to redden CI.
(4) Nit: delay.ts:107 names the guard `pattern-3-face-pin.test.ts`; the file is `art/scenarios/pattern3-face-pin.test.ts` (no hyphen). One-character comment fix while you are in the file.

**New params:**
- `time_cv_amt` — min -1, max +1, curve 'linear', defaultValue +1, no units, label 'Time CV'. WHY (a documented DEAD FEATURE, not a nice-to-have): delay.ts:159 advertises "patch a slow LFO into the TIME CV jack at a few milliseconds of depth and you have a flanger/chorus" — and the module cannot do it. The jack's throw is FIXED by `cvScale: {mode:'linear'}` at ±(2 − 0.001)/2 = ±0.9995 s (cv-scale.ts:57-63 `halfSpan`), so the SMALLEST gesture a full-scale LFO can make is a half-second sweep. 'A few milliseconds of depth' is ~0.005 on this knob; today there is no knob. RANGE ±1, not 0..1, so it is an ATTENUVERTER: negative INVERTS the CV, which turns a rising envelope into a pitch DIVE instead of a climb, and costs nothing (a GainNode multiply). DEFAULT +1 is an EXACT identity multiply, so every saved rack, every existing patch and the ART profile are bit-identical. MIGRATION: no saved node has this key; the factory must read `node.params?.time_cv_amt ?? 1` and the reconciler only calls setParam for keys that CHANGED (reconciler.ts:182-186), so a missing key never writes.
- `format` on `time_cv_amt` (PF-3) — `(v) => v === 0 ? 'off' : (v > 0 ? '+' : '-') + Math.round(Math.abs(v) * 100) + '%'`. A bare `-0.65` hides the two facts that matter on an attenuverter: that the sign is an INVERSION, and that 1.00 is identity. Pure, total, allocation-light (KnobConic.svelte:162 calls it every animation frame). ASCII '-' on purpose, NOT U+2212: the readout lands in a pinned VRT baseline and a minus-sign glyph is a font-fallback risk. ⚠ This makes delay the FIRST def-side consumer of PF-3 anywhere in the repo (grep `format: (` over packages/web/src/lib/audio/modules/*.ts → zero hits; `landmarks:` → zero; `options:` → filter only). The path is fully wired and unit-tested (knob-vocabulary-model.ts:84 `knobReadout`, KnobConic.svelte:160-162,282-283, testid `readout-time_cv_amt`; ModuleShell.svelte:545-547 passes it through), and the persistent readout is gated on options/landmarks/format being present so no other baseline moves. TASTE CALL WITH A ONE-LINE REVERT: delete the `format` key to match filter's un-formatted `cutoff_cv_amt`/`res_cv_amt` and NOTHING else moves — the readout only lands on the dock band (whose baseline is moving anyway) and the `full` lane tile (which has no independent baseline).
- NO other params. Diffing the def against the DSP per recipe Step 8: there is no DSP — the factory is three GainNodes and a native DelayNode (delay.ts:181-232), and all three params map 1:1 onto it. ZERO un-exposed capability; that is a finding, not a shortfall. Explicitly REJECTED on the module's own stated boundary (delay.ts:63-70, :159): feedback CV (the ABSENCE of it is what makes the 0.95 ceiling absolute — 'nothing patchable can push it past the clamp'), a tone control in the loop (that is COFEFVE), ping-pong / stereo (COFEFVE), tempo sync (COFEFVE).

**Control-loss ledger:**
- NONE from the legacy card — verified line by line, adversarially, per recipe Step 7. DelayCard.svelte is 43 lines total: ModuleTitle, PatchPanel, and exactly three <Fader>s (:33 time, :34 feedback, :35 mix). Grep for `<button|<select|<input|oncontextmenu|manualTrigger|Toggle|Selector|format` returns ZERO hits. All three faders map 1:1 onto ranked params. The plan's §5 ledger row ('mixer · delay · reverb · vca · adsr · shimmershine · tomtom — NONE, verified line by line') is CORRECT for delay.
- REVERSE loss, and it must be named: `time_cv_amt` will NOT appear on DelayCard.svelte (the legacy lane card). Where it goes: (a) the curated DOCK faceplate, which is reachable with `?shell=1` OFF — DockFullView mounts the migrated shell on `migrated(type)` = STRICT_FACES membership alone (Canvas.svelte:7944 → strict-faces.ts:69 → DockFullView.svelte:188), no preview flag; (b) the group-bar exposable list, auto-synthesized as `param-time_cv_amt` (group-controls.ts); (c) MIDI-learn + automation, since it is a real ParamDef. PRECEDENT, already shipped: FilterCard.svelte renders only cutoff/res/mode — it does NOT render `cutoff_cv_amt`/`res_cv_amt` (filter.ts:118-119), which have lived dock-only since P1 batch 3. Taste call: if you want parity, add a 4th Fader to DelayCard.svelte; nothing in the spec depends on it.
- Fader caption 'Fb' (DelayCard.svelte:34) → the face reads 'Feedback' (the def label, delay.ts:93). An IMPROVEMENT, and already argued in the def at :86-91 ('label is COSMETIC … Fb was an unreadable abbreviation'). Not a loss.
- Card port captions 'IN'/'OUT' (DelayCard.svelte:23-24, passed as portsFromDef label overrides) are NOT reproduced on the rear card. `rearHoleLabel` finds no paramTarget and no `_cv` stem for port `audio`, so it falls through to `resolveVerboseLabel({id:'audio'})` (rear-card-model.ts:133-149). Label QUALITY, the PF-4/PF-5 class — not a control. Out of scope here; note it, do not fix it in this PR.
- DELIBERATELY DROPPED, with the reason: the 'output blend' page. It held exactly ONE knob and is a house template copied verbatim across four modules — cloudseed.ts:626 (4 controls), reverb.ts:99 (1), shimmershine.ts:271 (1), delay.ts:134 (1). The control (`mix`) is NOT dropped; only its band header is. Per the plan's own page-earning rule (§7 Step 4): a page earns a header only if it groups ≥2 controls or the lone control is the module's identity. `mix` is neither.

Delay's linux pairs were DRAINED and captured in P1 batch 3 (the comment at :1052-1058 records it).

⇒ **PLAN CORRECTION.** The plan's §6 VRT table (line 1299) lists Batch B's VRT work as `linux/face-qbrt-{compact,dock}` only — it enumerates DRAINS and silently omits RE-CAPTURES. delay needs no drain and DOES need a linux dispatch. A darwin-only regen leaves the stale linux `face-delay-dock` in the tree, and vrt-strict on CI (which runs linux) goes RED.

WHICH SCENES MOVE:
• `face-delay-dock` — MOVES, both platforms. An entire section band disappears (2 → 1), a fourth knob appears, and a cluster sub-header appears. Regenerate darwin locally (`task vrt:one -- delay`, then inspect the PNG diff); dispatch `flox activate -- gh workflow run vrt-update.yml -f ref=<branch> -f platform=linux` **UNSCOPED** (never `-f grep=…` — it dies as `startup_failure`). The bot's push lands follow-on runs in `action_required`; approve them.
• `face-delay-compact` — should be **PIXEL-IDENTICAL** on both platforms, and this is a real prediction, not a hope: the compact cap is `LANE_ROW_MAX_CELLS_WITH_GLYPH = 2` (module-shell-model.ts:286, curated-face.ts:77) and ranks 1-2 are `time, feedback` before AND after. The tile never reads `pages`. **Treat a compact diff as a FINDING to investigate, not a baseline to accept.**

THE TRAP TO PRE-EMPT (CLAUDE.md / the A2 #1213 precedent): `DOCK_MAX_DIFF = 1500 px` (workflow-shell-faces.spec.ts:74) and `--update-snapshots` only rewrites a snapshot whose comparison FAILS. Removing an ~81 px band + adding a knob + a sub-header should be far over 1500, but MEASURE the darwin diff before dispatching. If it comes in under tolerance, `git rm e2e/vrt/__screenshots__/workflow-shell-faces.spec.ts/linux/face-delay-dock.png` FIRST — Playwright always writes a MISSING snapshot. A green dispatch that commits nothing is a RED FLAG, never 'nothing to do'.

EXEMPTION LEDGER: **no drains, and do NOT add an exemption.** The vrt-meta linux-deficit ratchet counts `EXEMPT_BASELINE_PAIRS` entries starting with `linux/` (vrt-meta.test.ts:314-318), not files on disk — so a `git rm` + re-capture does not touch it, but parking the pair would RAISE a ceiling that only shrinks.

**Risks (as authored):**
- PF-12 IS NOT LANDED AND IS A HARD BLOCKER. VERIFIED: `AudioDomainNodeHandle.inputs` is `Map<string, {node: AudioNode; input: number; param?: AudioParam}>` (engine.ts:38) — there is no `attenuate` field, and the string 'attenuate' appears NOWHERE in packages/web/src/lib/audio. The CV→AudioParam branch (engine.ts:397-462) connects the WaveShaper output STRAIGHT to `din.param` (:456) with no interposition seam. Do not open the delay face PR before PF-12 merges. Size it S (~12 LOC) as the plan says, but see the next risk for the part that is not 12 LOC.
- THE PF-12 TAP MOVE IS THE PART THAT SILENTLY NO-OPS, and a naive test cannot see it. engine.ts:461-462 taps `connectSource` — the SAME node fed to `din.param` at :456. If the analyser tap is left upstream of the attenuator, the motorized modulation readout on TIME reports the PRE-attenuation CV forever, and a 'byte-identical at gain 1.0' regression test is INVARIANT to exactly that bug (the CLAUDE.md instrument-validation rule: a metric blind to the dimension under test returns a clean number). TEST AT GAIN 0.5 and assert the tapped value HALVED — that is the negative control.
- THE FILTER PRECEDENT DOES NOT TRANSFER, and the plan never mentions filter at all. filter ALREADY ships two attenuverters (`cutoff_cv_amt`/`res_cv_amt`, filter.ts:118-119, contract-lock.txt:1118,1120) with NO platform change — but only because filter's CV inputs are PLAIN NODE inputs into a ChannelMerger (`['cutoff', {node: cutoffAtten, input: 0}]`, filter.ts:253-254) with no `param:` and no `cvScale` (filter.ts:74-82 explains the deliberate omission). delay's `time` input is the OPPOSITE shape: `{node: delay, input: 0, param: delay.delayTime}` (delay.ts:228) on the engine's CV→AudioParam fast path. Copying filter onto delay means DROPPING `cvScale: {mode:'linear'}` and hand-rolling the scaling, which (a) MODIFIES contract-lock.txt:788 instead of leaving it alone, (b) DELETES the knob-centred, min/max-CLAMPED LUT — `buildCvCurve` clamps `effective` into [0.001, 2] BEFORE subtracting the knob (cv-scale.ts:57-63, 196-206), so today a −1 CV at the 0.25 default bottoms out at 1 ms; after the rewire it would drive delayTime to a DelayNode-clamped 0 and degenerate the feedback loop — an AUDIBLE behaviour change for every saved rack with a cable in TIME, and (c) falsifies the authored `docs.inputs.time` sentence about the curve being baked at patch time (delay.ts:164). REJECT unless the owner explicitly wants that behaviour change. ZERO-PLATFORM-CHANGE FALLBACK if PF-12 slips: keep the AudioParam path and expose `param: cvBus.gain` on an always-on ConstantSource(1) → cvBus → depthGain(time_cv_amt) → delay.delayTime. That preserves the LUT byte-for-byte but leaves the tap pre-attenuation and costs a ConstantSource per instance. Recommend PF-12; document the fallback.
- ART: the `.f32` MUST NOT MOVE, the `.sha` MUST. The `docs-hash-ignore` markers wrap ONLY `face:` (delay.ts:97-154) and `docs:` (:156-179) — PF-11 has ALREADY LANDED here. The new ParamDef sits in `params:` (:85-95) and the attenuator sits in `factory` (:181-275), BOTH outside the markers, so `docsStrippedRepoSourceSha('.../modules/delay.ts')` (art/scenarios/delay/profile.test.ts:110; strip regex at art/setup/capture.ts:97-101) WILL move. The rendered audio must not: `renderOfflineDef` applies params at construction and patches only the `audio` input (art/setup/offline.ts:72,78) — the attenuator GainNode is dangling in the CV path with nothing connected. Re-pin the `.sha` LAST via `flox activate -- task art:update`, and confirm ONLY `art/baselines/delay/audio.sha` moved. `art/baselines/delay/audio.f32` and `fingerprints.generated.json` (whose `sourceSha256` is the sha of the .f32, not of the source) must be UNCHANGED. A moved `.f32`, a moved peakDb/rmsDb, or a moved spectrum means the attenuator leaked into the audio path — STOP, do not re-pin.
- PAGE-ID COLLISION — delay has one and it is live. The curated rear group id is `'signal'` (delay.ts:150). rearFieldPlan's voice/signal slot claims any group whose id is 'voice' or 'signal' (rear-card-model.ts:261-268) and the page loop at :285-299 does NOT check `usedGroupIds` before re-claiming a group whose id equals a page id — so a page named `'signal'` would build that band TWICE and redden the totality gate ('rear derivation rendered a port TWICE', module-face-lint.test.ts:761-766). This is dx7's 'voice' bug, same mechanism. USE `id: 'echo'`. The second curated group id `'mod'` matches no page id, so it falls to the extra-curated append (:302-306) — exactly one band, no collision.
- FIRST PRODUCTION CONSUMER OF A SHIPPED-BUT-UNUSED SEAM, and the gate is blind to half of it. `ModuleFacePage.clusters` (PF-9) has ZERO consumers today — a scan of every def's `pages:` block finds no front-side `clusters:`; all seven `clusters:` hits (adsr, dx7, karplus, kickdrum, snaredrum, sixstrum, tidy-vco) are `face.rear.clusters`. The render path exists (ModuleShell.svelte:792-801, `<h5 class="cluster-label">` + `data-face-cluster`) but has never been exercised by a real def in VRT or e2e. ⚠ faces-parity CANNOT catch a cluster whose sub-header fails to render: `dockPlanControls` flattens clusters into the SAME cell multiset as the un-clustered row (curated-face.ts:271-273), so a silently-headerless cluster balances every count. Add an explicit dock assertion on `[data-face-cluster="time cv depth"]`, or the only witness is the VRT PNG. ONE-LINE REVERT: delete the `clusters` key and the page renders four flat knobs — nothing else in the spec depends on it.
- VRT LINUX RE-CAPTURE — the plan's §6 VRT table OMITS it and a darwin-only regen ships red CI. See vrtImpact.
- DOCS MUST NOT RUN AHEAD OF BEHAVIOUR, and must not lag it either. `docs.inputs.time` (delay.ts:164) currently states '±1 moves the delay by up to ±1.0 s around the knob' — after the attenuverter that is '±1 × AMT'. Rewrite it in the SAME commit. KEEP the 'the scaling curve is baked when the cable is PATCHED' sentence — it stays TRUE under PF-12 (the LUT is untouched) and becomes FALSE only under the rejected filter-style rewire. `docs.controls.time_cv_amt` is REQUIRED: delay is in STRICT_DOCS (strict-docs.ts:71) and module-docs-lint.test.ts:151 fails a promoted module with an undocumented param.
- LATENT (not live) CARD↔DEF DIVERGENCE in DelayCard.svelte — see the note under rationale. Boy-scout fix belongs in this PR since we are already touching delay.
- The compact lane tile SHOULD be pixel-identical. If it moves, that is a FINDING, not a baseline to accept — see vrtImpact.

**Counts independently re-derived by the fact-checker:**

> INDEPENDENTLY DERIVED from packages/web/src/lib/audio/modules/delay.ts (276 lines):
• params = 3 — `time` (log 0.001..MAX_DELAY_S=2, default 0.25, units 's') :92, `feedback` (linear 0..MAX_FEEDBACK=0.95, default 0.4) :93, `mix` (linear 0..1, default 0.35) :94. MAX_DELAY_S=2 at :61, MAX_FEEDBACK=0.95 at :70.
• inputs = 2 — `audio` (audio) :79, `time` (cv, paramTarget='time', cvScale={mode:'linear'}) :80.
• outputs = 1 — `audio` (audio) :83.  ⇒ declared ports = 3.
• controlFamilies = 0, exposableControls = 0, face.paramCells = none, face.momentary = none, stereoPairs = none.
• face.order = 3 keys :131; face.pages = 2 :132-135 (`line`, `output`); face.glyph = 'meter' :136; face.rear.groups = 1, id 'signal' :150; face.rear.audioRate = ['time'] :151.
• contract-lock.txt delay block = 7 lines, :786-792.
• Legacy card DelayCard.svelte = 43 lines, 3 <Fader>s (:33-35), no buttons/selects/inputs.
AFTER the spec's proposal: 4 params, 3 ports, 1 page, 2 rear groups, 8 contract-lock lines.

AGREEMENT: the spec's "3 declared ports = 3 holes" matches. The spec's ciDelta "4 cells → 5" does NOT — delay has 3 params and 0 families, so faces-parity renders 3 cells today, 4 after.

**DEFECTS — fix these before building:**

- **[MAJOR]** ciDelta states faces-parity goes "4 cells → 5". The def has THREE params and zero control families, so the dock renders 3 cells today and 4 after adding time_cv_amt.
  - *evidence:* packages/web/src/lib/audio/modules/delay.ts:92-94 declares exactly three ParamDefs (time, feedback, mix); the def has no `controlFamilies`. e2e/tests/faces-parity.spec.ts:585-588 asserts `expect(cells.length).toBe(defIds.length + (spec.controlFamilies?.length ?? 0))` — a gate that is green on main, so delay's rendered cell count is provably 3. The plan's own CI table agrees: `.myrobots/plans/dx7-and-faces-design-program-2026-07-27.md:1325` reads "Batch B | qbrt +1, delay +1, reverb +1 | +2.4 s" (3 modules × 1 cell × 0.8 s). The derived +0.8 s survives; the absolute counts are both off by one.

- **[MINOR]** newParams says the factory "is three GainNodes and a native DelayNode (delay.ts:181-232)". It is FIVE GainNodes, and the cited range truncates the factory mid-map.
  - *evidence:* packages/web/src/lib/audio/modules/delay.ts:182 `inputGain`, :197 `dry`, :204 `feedback`, :206 `wet`, :218 `output` — five `ctx.createGain()` calls, plus `ctx.createDelay(MAX_DELAY_S)` at :202. The factory body runs :181-275; :232 lands inside the `outputs` Map literal. The spec contradicts itself: its ART risk bullet correctly cites "factory (:181-275)".

- **[MINOR]** reverb.ts:99 is cited for two mutually exclusive facts — as reverb's `order` leading with `mix`, and as reverb's one-control 'output blend' page.
  - *evidence:* packages/web/src/lib/audio/modules/reverb.ts:96 is `order: ['mix', 'size', 'damp']`; :99 is `{ id: 'output', label: 'output blend', controls: ['mix'] },`. The controlLoss citation ("reverb.ts:99 (1)") is right; the rank-3 rationale citation ("reverb.ts:99 leads with `mix`") points at the wrong line and should be :96. The parallel citations cloudseed.ts:626 (4 controls) and shimmershine.ts:271 (1 control) both check out exactly.

- **[MINOR]** The band label `echo · time · feedback · mix` does not follow the precedent the spec cites, and lists only 3 of the page's 4 controls.
  - *evidence:* .myrobots/plans/dx7-and-faces-design-program-2026-07-27.md:696 is the cited lfo precedent: `{ id:'engine', label:'rate · depth · shape' }` — the label is the MEMBER LIST ONLY; the page id is never repeated inside it. The spec prepends the id word ('echo ·') and then omits `time_cv_amt`, so the label is neither the cited style nor a complete member list. Nothing gates page-label text (module-face-lint.test.ts:193-206 checks only glyph validity and order duplicates), so this is style, not a build failure.

- **[MINOR]** "the SMALLEST gesture a full-scale LFO can make is a half-second sweep" contradicts the spec's own halfSpan arithmetic in the same sentence.
  - *evidence:* packages/web/src/lib/audio/cv-scale.ts:58-61 — `const halfSpan = (paramMax - paramMin) / 2; const effective = knob + cv * depth * halfSpan;` For delay's time (0.001..2) that is 0.9995, which the spec states correctly two clauses earlier as ±0.9995 s. A full-scale ±1 CV is therefore a ~1 s excursion each way (~2 s peak-to-peak), not "a half-second sweep". The dead-feature argument is unaffected; only the number is wrong.

- **[MINOR]** A cluster of line/path citations drift by a few lines or name the wrong directory. All resolve to the right code, but a reader following them lands off-target.
  - *evidence:* rear-card-model.ts: the page loop is :287-300 (spec: :285-299) and the extra-curated append :302-307 (spec: :302-306); `rearHoleLabel` is :137-152 (spec: :133-149). module-face-lint.test.ts: the "rear derivation rendered a port TWICE" push is :768 (spec: :761-766). `looksLikeToggle` is in packages/web/src/lib/graph/group-controls.ts:46-48 — correct lines, but there is no ui/workflow/group-controls.ts and the spec gives no path. ModuleShell.svelte lives at packages/web/src/lib/ui/modules/ (not ui/workflow/), and its cluster render is :798-799 (spec: :792-801). KnobConic.svelte builds the vocab at :160 and emits `readout-<paramId>` at :283 (spec: ":162 calls it every animation frame", ":282-283"). Contrast: engine.ts:38, :456, :461-462, Canvas.svelte:7944, module-docs-lint.test.ts:151, module-face-lint.test.ts:419/:677, rear-card-model.test.ts pins (tidyVco :55, kickdrum :120, adsr :158, vca :182, lfo :195, cloudseed :210), art/scenarios/delay/profile.test.ts:110, contract-lock.txt:786-792/:1118, vrt-exemptions.ts:1004-1017, workflow-shell-faces.spec.ts:62/:74/:222 are all EXACT.

- **[MINOR]** `order` and `pages` rank `time_cv_amt`, which does not exist in the def. This is a declared new param, not an invention — but it makes the face unbuildable until the param lands in the SAME commit, and the spec never states that dependency as a hard ordering constraint the way it does for PF-12.
  - *evidence:* packages/web/src/lib/audio/modules/delay.ts:85-95 declares only time/feedback/mix. module-face-lint.test.ts:125-148 pushes an orphan for any `face.order` key that fails `keyResolves`, and :212-243 (STRICT_FACES completeness) fails any promoted module whose params are not all ranked. delay is in STRICT_FACES (strict-faces.ts:50) and STRICT_DOCS (strict-docs.ts:71), so the param, its `face.order` rank, its page membership AND its `docs.controls` entry (module-docs-lint.test.ts:151) must all land atomically or three unit gates go red.


---

## reverb

**Verdict: CONTRADICTS_CODE** · archetype: FX / mono insert processor — the "wet-dry blend at rank 1" FX archetype (cloudseed.ts:553, shimmershine.ts:249 name it verbatim). Mono in / mono out, ZERO CV ports, no families, no statics, no momentary, no paramCells. It is the simplest face shape in STRICT_FACES: N knobs in one signal-order band + a meter glyph. reverb.ts:53-54 — inputs `[{id:'audio',type:'audio'}]`, outputs `[{id:'audio',type:'audio'}]`.

**Rationale (as authored):** WHAT IT IS FOR, in one sentence, because every rank descends from it: reverb is the utility room you patch when you refuse to make a decision — one fixed room, no CV, no menu — and the verb a player performs on it is "send some signal to it", not "design a space".

WHY THIS `order` — and I am saying plainly that ranks 1-3 are UNCHANGED from reverb.ts:96, because presenting an identical array as a redesign is the plan's own anti-pattern #3 (plan:1432). What changes is that rank 4 now exists and the arguments are now DSP-grounded rather than asserted.
• `mix` rank 1 is right for a reason stronger than "every FX has a mix": this module has no separate wet fader, so MIX *is* "how much reverb", AND it is a LOUDNESS control whether the player meant it or not — the Faust tank sums eight combs with no output scaling (reverb.dsp:20 → `re.mono_freeverb`), measured at +2.4 dB out at the 0.3 default, +11.4 dB fully wet, and a wet peak ~+21 dB over dry at size 1 (docs.controls.mix). It is the only control on the module that can clip a downstream stage, so it must survive to the 1-cell mini tile.
• `size` rank 2: the decay TIME, measured T60 0.27 s → 3.6 s at default damp (docs.controls.size). Biggest perceptual mover.
• `damp` rank 3, and the demotion is defensible precisely because it would be WRONG for shimmershine: there, `damp` is rank 2 in Batch D because at damp 1 the comb store stops taking signal and the tail collapses whatever DECAY and SIZE do (plan:849-852) — it is a panic button. Here the same knob is bounded tone-plus-second-decay (measured tail balance +6.8 dB → −15.3 dB across the travel), set-and-forget on a three-knob room. Same param name, opposite rank, and the argument is the DSP's, not taste.
• `diffusion` rank 4: the texture control, the one with no established mental model, and the only one whose default is a historical constant rather than a design choice. Ranking it last is also the conservative read on the glyph cliff — though note the cliff is caused by there being a fourth control at all, not by which one it is.

Read the ladder back as a sentence: mini = MIX (how much room, and the loudness knob). Compact = + SIZE (how long it rings) + the live meter. Plate = + DAMP + DIFFUSION, and the meter drops. Dock = all four in signal order under one header, plus the hero meter.

WHY ONE PAGE, IN SIGNAL ORDER, WHILE `order` LEADS WITH MIX. They disagree on purpose and the face comment must say so or the next author "fixes" one to match the other (plan:1387). `order` answers "if I can only show you N controls, which N?" — that is a priority question and the answer is level-critical-first. `pages` answers "you can see everything; in what order does it make sense?" — that is a signal-flow question, and the signal is unambiguous: input → 8 parallel lowpass-feedback combs (`size` = fb1, `damp` = the one-pole inside each loop) → sum → 4 series Schroeder allpasses (`diffusion` = fb2) → linear dry/wet crossfade (`mix`). So `size, damp, diffusion, mix`, which is exactly reversed from the priority ranking at both ends. That is the clearest illustration in Batch B of why the two arrays are allowed to disagree.

WHY THAT HEADER TEXT. Band headers on this rig teach (`gain = base + cv × amount` on vca; `filter · dual mono` on qbrt). Reverb's single most consequential patch-time fact is the one its own docs repeat three times and its face comment repeats once: the wet leg is not level-matched. `'reverb tank → blend · the wet leg runs +10..+18 dB'` states the flow, keeps the word `tank` that ties to shimmershine, and puts the hazard where a dock user is standing. I used the RANGE +10..+18 rather than the plan's single "~+10 dB" because +10 dB is only true at default SIZE; +18 dB is the measured figure at SIZE 1 (reverb.ts:22-24), and SIZE-wide-open is exactly when a player is about to get hurt.

NO CLUSTERS. `ModuleFacePage.clusters` has landed (graph/types.ts:503) and would be the tool if this band were long. It is four cells. Three sub-headers over four knobs (`combs` / `allpass` / `blend`) is more furniture than content — the header already carries the flow arrow. Reach for a cluster when controls are the same idea twice (graph/types.ts:491-496); these are one idea, once.

REAR: CHANGE NOTHING. `{ groups: [{ id: 'signal', label: 'mono in', ports: ['audio'] }] }` (reverb.ts:110-112) is finished, and I traced the page collapse through `rearFieldPlan` (rear-card-model.ts:198-333) to be sure it survives: reverb has ZERO per-param CV inputs, so `byPage` is empty, every page slot produces a 0-hole band, and "empty bands are dropped" (`:102`, enforced at `:297`). The plan = one band (`signal` / 'mono in' / [audio]) + the outputs rail, holeCount 2, identical before and after. `rear-card-model.test.ts` has no reverb pin (grepped), and there is no `rear-reverb` VRT scene (only rear-dx7/sixstrum/tidyVco/vca exist). ⚠ AND CHECK THE COLLISION RULE THE PLAN WARNS ABOUT: rear group id is `'signal'`, page id is `'tank'` — no collision, so no band renders twice. Do NOT be tempted to rename the merged page to `'signal'` to match the header's subject; that is the exact dx7 `voice` trap (plan's rule) and it would render the band twice and redden the rear-derivation totality gate.

```ts
face: {
  order: ["mix", "size", "damp", "diffusion"],
  pages: [
    { id: "tank", label: "reverb tank \u2192 blend \u00b7 the wet leg runs +10..+18 dB", controls: ["size", "damp", "diffusion", "mix"] },
  ],
  glyph: "meter \u2014 UNCHANGED (reverb.ts:101). The existing in-source argument still holds and I re-verified its premise: an RMS meter is the one glyph that reads a decay at 84 px, and it doubles as the clip warning for the unscaled wet leg. The `tail` glyph (scrolling RMS history) proposed for this module is DEMOTED at plan:117 for three reasons I confirmed against the code: (a) it swaps a 16 px VuMeter dock hero for a 64 px ScopeScreen-family hero, +48 px on a face that just spent a band; (b) ScopeScreen maps buffers to a centred \u00b11 trace (ScopeScreen.svelte:31) and an RMS history is positive-only 0..1 \u2014 it would draw in the top half; (c) getLevel() is RMS over GLYPH_TAP_FFT_SIZE=2048 \u2248 43 ms, and reverb's own first wet energy arrives at ~25 ms (docs.explanation), so the glyph could not resolve this module's onset anyway. \u26a0 PRICED LOSS: at the `full` (in-lane plate) tier the glyph DISAPPEARS. laneBodyPlan (module-shell-model.ts:340-342): cellCount = min(4,6) = 4, rows = ceil(4/3) = 2, `glyph = hasGlyph && rows <= 1` \u2192 false. Today reverb has 3 controls \u2192 rows 1 \u2192 glyph shown. Adding ANY 4th ranked control costs the full-tier glyph; it is not a consequence of ranking diffusion last. The meter survives at mini, compact and the dock hero. NO GATE SEES THIS \u2014 module-face-lint.test.ts:677 compares only cellCount (4 selected == 4 rendered), never the glyph flag \u2014 so it must be stated in the face comment and the commit body or it gets rediscovered as a bug.",
}
```

**New params:**
- `diffusion` — allpass feedback, currently the hardcoded literal `fb2 = 0.5` at packages/dsp/src/reverb.dsp:18. **RANGE: `min: 0, max: 0.95, curve: 'linear', defaultValue: 0.5`, label `'Diffusion'`.** ⚠ **THE PLAN'S IMPLIED 0..1 IS A BUG — DO NOT SHIP IT.** Faust's `re.mono_freeverb` passes `-fb2` as `allpass_comb`'s coefficient; that allpass is H(z) = (b0 + z^-D)/(1 + b0·z^-D) with poles at z^D = fb2, i.e. pole radius fb2^(1/D). At fb2 = 1.0 the poles sit ON the unit circle and one of them is at z = 1 — a DC INTEGRATOR. The comb stage upstream is a lowpass-feedback comb that passes DC, so diffusion = 1.0 gives unbounded DC drift through four series stages on a module whose own docs promise 'the tail always dies — 0.95 is the ceiling' (reverb.ts:20, docs.controls.size). 0.95 mirrors the ceiling `size` already has (`fb1 = 0.5 + 0.45*size` → 0.50..0.95, reverb.ts:18) so the docs sentence stays true for BOTH feedback paths and the number is already a measured precedent in this file. The low end IS safe and musical: at 0 the allpasses degenerate to pure delays (225/341/441/556 samples ≈ 5.1/7.7/10/12.6 ms) — discrete flutter-echo, a real sound, not a degenerate one.
- `.dsp` edit — ONE line, IDENTITY MAPPING, NO SMOOTHING: replace `fb2 = 0.5;` (reverb.dsp:18) with `fb2 = hslider("diffusion[style:knob]", 0.5, 0.0, 0.95, 0.001);`. Identity (not `a + b*x` like `size`) is deliberate and it is the byte-identity argument: the value handed to `mono_freeverb` at the default is literally the float32 `0.5f` — the exact immediate the literal produced — so there is no arithmetic to reason about, and every constant fold Faust could have performed on the old literal (`-g`, `1-g`) is exact at 0.5 anyway. **Do NOT `si.smoo` it** (plan:748-752 is right, and I verified the premise): `si.smooth`'s state initialises at 0 and ramps over ~23 ms, and the ART capture starts at sample 0 with a 0.1 s burst — a ramp would rewrite the first ~1100 samples of the pinned `.f32`. ZIPPER TRADE, quantified so nobody has to guess: Faust reads an hslider once per 128-sample compute() = one step every 2.67 ms; a full-travel drag in ~1 s steps the coefficient by ~0.0025 per block, an output discontinuity of Δg·(x − y[n−D]) ≈ −52 dBFS. Inaudible in a reverb wash. If the owner does hear it, the follow-up is `si.smoothAndInit(ba.tau2pole(0.02), 0.5)` (initialises AT 0.5, so byte-identity survives) in its own PR — I could NOT verify that primitive exists in this repo's pinned compiler (dist json reports libfaust 2.78.3 via @grame/faustwasm ^0.7.0; the flox `faust2` 2.85.5 is NOT the wasm build path — packages/dsp/scripts/build.mjs:33-35 shells faust2wasm.js from the npm package).
- `landmarks` on `diffusion` (PF-10, contract-transparent, graph/types.ts:398) — `[{value:0,label:'flutter'},{value:0.5,label:'classic'},{value:0.95,label:'smear'}]`. Argument: it is the ONLY control on this module with no established mental model and no unit — mix/size/damp are all 'more or less of an obvious thing' — and 0.5 is Jezar's historical Freeverb constant, which is worth naming. ⚠ LABELS MUST NAME REGIONS, NOT POINTS: `knobReadout` (knob-vocabulary-model.ts:84-89) resolves landmarks by `nearestByValue` with NO tolerance, so 'classic' prints across [0.25, 0.725]. `flutter`/`classic`/`smear` are region-honest; a label like `'freeverb 0.5'` would be a lie for half the travel. Gate-clean: param-vocabulary.test.ts requires landmarks NOT be `curve:'discrete'` (✓ linear), values inside [min,max] (✓ 0/0.5/0.95), unique + non-blank (✓), and forbids options+landmarks together (✓ no options). It adds a `readout-diffusion` testid, which is invisible to faces-parity (that gate matches `[data-testid^="control-"]`, faces-parity.spec.ts:555). ONE-LINE REVERT: delete the `landmarks:` array; nothing else moves except the dock baseline, which is moving anyway.
- NOTHING ELSE. `reverb.dsp` has exactly three `hslider`s and one hidden constant; after this PR the def↔DSP surface is 1:1 with zero un-exposed capability. The plan's PF-3 row (plan:96) lists `reverb` as a `ParamDef.format` consumer — **that dependency is unsubstantiated and I am rejecting it.** §4's reverb spec proposes no `format` anywhere, and every candidate is an invalid instrument: a T60-seconds readout on `size` would be a lie whenever `damp` ≠ 0.3 (docs.controls.damp measures T60 falling 4.4 s → 1.16 s across damp at fixed size), and a dB readout on `mix` is size- AND damp-dependent for the same reason. `format` receives ONE param's value; both readouts would be blind to the dimension that actually moves them. Reverb takes no `format`.

**Control-loss ledger:**
- FROM THE FACE: **NONE.** I re-read ReverbCard.svelte line by line (39 lines total). It renders exactly three `<Fader>`s (`:29-31` size/damp/mix) inside a `<PatchPanel>` (`:27`) with a `<ModuleTitle>` (`:25`). Grepping the card for `<button|<select|<input|oncontextmenu|manualTrigger|Toggle|Selector|format` returns ZERO hits. Every affordance the card has is a ranked param. The plan's §5 ledger row (plan:1197, 'mixer · delay · reverb · vca · adsr · shimmershine · tomtom — NONE, verified line by line') is CORRECT for reverb.
- **⚠ NEW LOSS THIS PR CREATES, IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION — the plan misses it entirely.** `diffusion` lands in the def but NOT on `ReverbCard.svelte`, and the legacy card is still LIVE: legacy-fallback.ts:32-38 renders `'legacy'` — the module's own *Card.svelte, verbatim — in **dawless mode ALWAYS**, and in workflow mode when the shell preview is off. So a dawless user gets a def-declared knob with no control anywhere. **NO GATE CATCHES THIS**: module-docs-lint's card grep (`:231-248`) only covers `controlFamilies.testidPrefix`, never ParamDefs; module-face-lint reads the def; faces-parity reads the SHELL. REMEDY (mandatory, in this PR): add a fourth `<Fader ... label="Diffusion" min={0} max={0.95} defaultValue={0.5} curve="linear" paramId="diffusion" />` to `.fader-row` (ReverbCard.svelte:28-32) and widen `.reverb-card` from 200 px (`:37`); the three Faders are `display:inline-flex` (Fader.svelte:531) inside a plain block row with `padding: 0 18px`, so a fourth at 200 px will crowd or wrap. Adopt ShimmershineCard.svelte:41-47's explicit `display:flex; justify-content:center; gap:12px` row — 280 px holds five, so ~250 px holds four comfortably.
- **⚠ SECOND HAZARD OF THE SAME CLASS, pre-existing.** `ReverbCard.svelte:29-31` HARDCODES all four numeric facts per Fader (`min={0} max={1} defaultValue={0.5}` + `curve="linear"` + `label="Size"`), duplicating the def. I checked every one against reverb.ts:56-58 and they currently AGREE — so this is NOT today a live bug, and I am reporting it as a latent one, not a finding. But it is precisely the backdraft `camTiltX` class CLAUDE.md legislates against ('a control's range must come from ONE place'), and it is about to be exercised: the moment `diffusion` arrives with a NON-1.0 max, a hand-typed `max={1}` on the card would let the classic surface write 1.0 into the DC-integrator region the def forbids, and the model would clamp it silently. FIX WHILE YOU ARE IN THERE: drive the card's four Faders off `reverbDef.params` (the card already imports `reverbDef` at `:5` and already uses it for defaults at `:14-16`), so the numbers exist once. The migrated shell already does this correctly — ModuleShell.svelte:529-546 passes `min={pd.min} max={pd.max} defaultValue={pd.defaultValue} curve={pd.curve} label={pd.label}` straight from the def.
- **`size` → `Decay` RELABEL: I RECOMMEND *NOT* DOING IT, against plan:740-743 and plan:1455 (§8.6).** It is free in contract terms (label is excluded — verified), and the motivation is real (docs.controls.size has to open with 'The decay TIME, despite the name'). But the plan contradicts itself: reverb.ts:84-87's face comment deliberately shares vocabulary with SHIMMERSHINE because shimmershine IS this tank plus a regeneration loop — and shimmershine has BOTH a `size` AND a `decay` param that mean DIFFERENT things (`effSize = size * (0.5 + 0.5*decay)`, plan:853). Renaming reverb's `size` to `Decay` means the knob a player learns as DECAY in reverb is the knob called SIZE in shimmershine, and the knob called DECAY in shimmershine is a scaler that does not exist here. That is worse than the name it fixes. If the owner wants it anyway it belongs in a cross-module vocabulary PR that renames both coherently, after Batch D. ⚠ AND IT CARRIES THE #1213 TRAP: `KnobConic` always renders `<div class="label">{label}</div>` (KnobConic.svelte:277) at 9 px uppercase mono, so at compact-tile zoom 0.45 'SIZE'→'DECAY' is roughly 25×5 px of a 88×82 = 7216 px capture — comfortably UNDER `COMPACT_MAX_DIFF = 150` (workflow-shell-faces.spec.ts:73). The VRT gate would pass-but-stale and `--update-snapshots` would write NOTHING. If it ships, `git rm` both `face-reverb-compact.png` files first. THE FREE 90%: rewrite `docs.controls.size` to LEAD with decay (plan:743 — do this regardless, it costs nothing and moves no gate) and let the band header carry the module's real hazard.
- NOT LOST, DELIBERATELY DROPPED: the `output blend` PAGE (reverb.ts:99). Its single control MIX moves into the merged `tank` band, not off the face. Reason is the recipe's own rule (plan:1389): a page earns a header only if it groups ≥2 controls or the lone control is the module's identity, and MIX — a dry/wet crossfade — is the least module-specific control in the rack. It is NOT a budget merge: I measured the committed baseline rather than computing it, per plan:1429. `face-reverb-dock.png` is 1220×398 against a `.dock-faceplate` cap of `min(60vh,680px)` = 425 px in the VRT viewport (vrt-exemptions.ts:1026-1033), i.e. ~27 px of real headroom today. The merge buys ~81 px it does not need; it is bought for the grouping argument alone. ⚠ The furniture tie to SHIMMERSHINE survives via the page **id** `'tank'` and the membership SHAPE — Batch D gives shimmershine `{tank: size, decay, mix}` (plan:857), i.e. its blend also folds into `tank`. But Batch B ships BEFORE Batch D, so between the two merges reverb's face comment must NOT claim runtime parity with shimmershine's current three bands — write the tie as a Batch-D forward reference or the comment is false on merge day.

**Contract delta (claimed):** EXACTLY +1 line, and I verified where it lands. contract-lock.txt today (lines 2659-2664): `reverb meta domain=audio` / `reverb in audio audio` / `reverb out audio audio` / `reverb param damp 0..1 linear default=0.3` / `reverb param mix 0..1 linear default=0.3` / `reverb param size 0..1 linear default=0.5`. serializeModuleContract (contract-signature.ts:105-108) sorts params by `id.localeCompare`, so damp < diffusion < mix < size and the new line is INSERTED at 2663, pushing mix/size down: `reverb param diffusion 0..0.95 linear default=0.5` (`num()` is `String(n)`, so 0.95 renders "0.95" and 0 renders "0"). 6 → 7 reverb lines. ⚠ The plan's contract table (plan:1247) says "+1 diffusion" and is CORRECT, but its §4 text never states the range — if diffusion ships as 0..1 the line reads `0..1` and the DC-integrator hazard above is in the contract. NOTHING ELSE MOVES: `face` has no branch in serializeModuleContract (plan:1256-1260, verified — contract-lock.txt contains zero `face` lines), so the order re-rank, the page collapse, the page label and the landmarks are all contract-transparent; `ParamDef.label` is likewise excluded, and `landmarks` is a whitelisted-out field (the projection reads only id/min/max/curve/defaultValue/units). ATTEST: NIL — reverb is an audio def, outside the WebGL basis; no `docs-hash-ignore` markers needed, no collab/grand basis file touched. Accept loop: `flox activate -- task docs:accept`, then review the one-line diff.

The compact tile shows `faceTierCap('compact', hasGlyph=true)` = `LANE_ROW_MAX_CELLS_WITH_GLYPH` = 2 cells (module-shell-model.ts:286) = ranks 1-2 = `mix, size` + the glyph.
